# Vossie Selling (Sample Mode) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build stage F as the spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-selling-design.md` describes: a student applies to sell through a five-step wizard and waits for the Hub; an approved seller sees a dashboard with this week's numbers and their badge's progress, lists, edits, pauses, marks sold and deletes listings with photos, and edits their shop; new listings appear in browse, on their page, on the storefront and in collections straight away; and the listing page and storefront recognise their owner. Everything runs on stages D and E's sample store, every form works without the app's JavaScript, and the only new client code is the listing form's photo field, on its two routes. Tested at five screen sizes and within the JS budget.

**Architecture:**
- **Listings and sellers move into D's sample store** (spec 2.1): four new tables, `sellers`, `listings`, `photos` and `onboardingDrafts`, seeded from the fixtures, which stay as they are. `lib/data/catalog.ts` turns rows into the public `Listing` and `Seller` shapes and decides what buyers see. Every cached function in `lib/data/index.ts` keeps its name and signature, reads the store through the catalog, and is tagged `listings` and `sellers` with `cacheTag`. Every write calls `updateTag`, then `refresh()`, then a `replace` redirect (E's pattern), so the next request sees the change and the browser drops its kept pages.
- **A shop's approval status stays on D's seller profile,** the account's link to its shop (plan decision 1). The shop's details live on its `sellers` row, keyed by a slug made when the application is sent. A sample shop with no owner account is always open.
- **Plain forms, Server Actions, redirects.** The wizard, the listing form, the shop form and every row button post to Server Actions in `lib/selling/shop-actions.ts` and `lib/selling/listing-actions.ts`. Each takes identity only from the session, checks the shop is the caller's, validates on the server, keeps errors and typed values in D's form state, and ends with `done()`.
- **Photos without a new dependency** (spec 2.3 and 2.4, decision 59). The server checks each file's signature and strips its metadata (`lib/photos/strip-metadata.ts`, JPEG, PNG and WebP). Photos live in the store and are served by `/photos/[id]`; `next/image` resizes them through the same `Photo` component. On the listing form, one small client component, `PhotoField`, shrinks and re-encodes photos in the browser and moves them without a round trip; without its JavaScript the same buttons post the form and the server does the work.
- **The owner's view costs nothing on the client.** The listing page's and storefront's session links (stage E's `ViewerMessageLink`) learn one more case: their owner gets **Edit listing** or **Edit shop**, streamed in from the server with `prefetch={false}`.

**Tech Stack:** As stage E leaves it:
- Next.js 16.4.0 with Cache Components and Partial Prefetching, React 19.3, TypeScript 5.9;
- Tailwind CSS 4.3 through PostCSS;
- pnpm 10.30;
- Vitest 5 with Testing Library and jsdom 30;
- Playwright 1.64 with axe-core.

**Sources:**
- **Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-selling-design.md`. "Spec 2.5" in this plan means that spec's section 2, decision 5; "spec 3" means its section 3.
- **The stages this builds on, planned but not built when this plan was written:** stage D, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-accounts.md` ("D's plan", "D Task 4", "D decision 3") and its spec; stage E, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-messaging.md` ("E's plan", "E Task 7", "E decision 2") and its spec, section 9 included.
- **The spec that builds on this one** (its needs shaped the tables): `2026-10-14-staff-tools-design.md` (stage G: approve, decline, pause and verify shops; hide listings; Featured from approved shops; mentors).
- **Earlier specs** (tokens, breakpoints, motion, accessibility, budgets; the listing model): `2026-10-08-homepage-design.md`, `2026-10-13-browse-and-listing-design.md` (6.1, the listing model) and `2026-10-13-shopping-pages-design.md` (the storefront).
- **The old demo:** `docs/reference/FUNCTION-MAP.md`, section G (selling, the listing form's fields) and section 6 (the limits).
- **Decisions:** `docs/reference/LANDING-DECISIONS.md`, rows 54 to 59 (59: photos without a new dependency) and 63 (Turbopack's `requestCost`, the JS room it bought).

## Before you start

**This plan starts after stage E.** When it was written (2026-10-14), stage D's build had just begun in the working tree (`826a033` amended D's plan) and stage E had a committed plan (`3b0a51a`) and no code. Start only once E's Task 17 (its docs) is committed. Then work through "Recheck before starting" below before Task 1: this plan is written against D's and E's planned file names and APIs, and the builds may have changed some.

**Approvals Lama gives in chat before execution starts:**
1. **Git.** Implementers leave their work uncommitted. The controller checks each task and commits it (Lama's standing choice). `vossie-audit/` and `.superpowers/` stay out of git and untouched. `.claude/launch.json` has a change made outside this work: never stage it.
2. **Execution.** Subagent-driven, with one combined reviewer per build step (plan check first, then code quality), time-boxed. This is Lama's standing preference.
3. **Files this plan removes or moves:** none. No task runs `rm`. Two files become unused and stay, listed for Lama in Task 18: `components/ComingSoon.tsx` and its test (the selling pitch replaces its last use).
4. **Two `next.config.ts` changes** (Task 6): Server Actions take bodies up to 32 MB (`experimental.serverActions.bodySizeLimit`, spec 2.3), and the image optimizer keeps no copies on disk (`images.maximumDiskCacheSize: 0`, plan decision 12).
5. **No new environment variables, cookies or browser-storage keys.** Selling uses D's session and form state, so the privacy page's `vossie_…` guard has nothing new to name.
6. **Sample wording.** The pitch, the waiting page, the dashboard's lines, the hints, errors, notices and empty states, and Lerato's sample shop are for Lama to change. Plan decision 27 lists where they live.
7. **A Playwright project that runs last** (Task 16): the end-to-end tests that publish, change or delete listings run after every other test, so the counts other tests check never move under them (plan decision 25).

No installs are needed. `sharp`, which the image optimizer uses, already comes with Next (recheck item 26).

**Global safety rules (from `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, highest priority):**
- Check before acting (`ls`, `git status`), as a separate tool call from the action.
- Run git commands one at a time. Never run destructive git commands, never amend, rebase or reset, never touch `.git/` internals, and never `rm` anything.
- Never kill an install or build that is still running.
- Port 3100 is Lama's dev server. Don't touch it. The e2e tests use 3200; the "looking at it" checks use 3201.
- Before you edit a shared file (`app/globals.css`, `next.config.ts`, `vitest.setup.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, `app/privacy/page.tsx`, `tests/e2e/*.spec.ts`, `docs/*`, and every file stages D and E created), read its current text and edit by the exact lines that are there now. Never paste back a whole list or file from an older copy. Where this plan quotes a line from D's or E's plan and the built file says it differently, edit by meaning and note it in the hand-over.

Limit 153,600 B. D's slots and E's links come before this plan; take E's final figures as the start |
| Data | `lib/data/index.ts`: every function is `"use cache"` with no `cacheTag` or `cacheLife`, reading the fixtures (`listings`, `sellers`, `pickupPoints` and the rest). "Switching to Supabase changes only this file" |
| Fixtures | 38 listings (`l-001`…, `fs-001`…; `l-016` paused, `l-026` sold), 29 sellers, 24 pickup points (2 per physical campus, none for Eduvos Online). Each listing's badge is its seller's, its cover is `/placeholders/listing/<category>.svg` with the title as alt, and `l-001` has two more photos. Fixtures import nothing from `lib` at run time (`scripts/make-placeholders.ts` runs them in Node) |
| Next 16.4 caching (read for this plan) | `cacheTag` throws outside a `"use cache"` scope (`server/use-cache/cache-tag.js`), so unit tests need a stand-in. `updateTag` works only in Server Actions and expires the tag at once: the next read waits for fresh data. A prerendered page carries the tags of the cached functions it used (`collectedTags` in `app-render.js`), so `updateTag("listings")` also expires every prerendered page that read listings. An id missing from `generateStaticParams` gets the App Shell first, then the rendered page, which Next keeps for later visits (`02-guides/incremental-static-regeneration-cache-components.md`). The default `"use cache"` life is 15 minutes' revalidation and no expiry |
| Server Actions | Bodies are capped at 1 MB unless `experimental.serverActions.bodySizeLimit` says otherwise; the limit counts multipart overhead. React renders a form whose action is a function as `method="POST" encType="multipart/form-data"`, so a file input works without JavaScript |
| Images | `next/image` optimizes local paths through `sharp` (present). It keeps resized copies in `.next/cache/images` for at least `minimumCacheTTL` (4 hours) or the source's `max-age`, whichever is longer, unless `images.maximumDiskCacheSize` is 0. SVG placeholders aren't optimized. `vitest.setup.ts` mocks `next/image`'s default export only |
| Listing page | `app/listings/[id]/page.tsx` awaits `params` inside `<Suspense fallback={null}>`, prerenders every id from `getListingIds()`, and calls `notFound()` for an unknown one. After E, `ListingActions` shows `MessageSellerLink` for an available listing and "See similar items" otherwise |
| Storefront | `app/sellers/[slug]/page.tsx` prerenders every slug from `getSellerSlugs()`. `SellerListings` shows "Nothing listed right now" when the seller has no available listings. After E, `SellerHero` has `MessageSellerLink` for a shop question |
| `/sell` | `app/sell/page.tsx` is `ComingSoon` ("Start selling"). The header and menu (`NavLink href="/sell"`), the footer and the homepage link to `/sell` with the default prefetch, so `/sell`'s route chunk is fetched on every page: it must stay free of client components |
| e2e counts | `browse.spec.ts` counts 36 listings at every campus, 14 at Midrand and 5 in Tech; `shopping.spec.ts` expects Tech corner's storefront to say "2 listings". Every test shares one server and one store |
| Privacy guard | `app/privacy/page.test.tsx` fails when a `"vossie_…"` name in the code isn't on the privacy page. This plan adds none |
| CSS | No class starts with `sell-`. `.photo` and `.photo-img` belong to the `Photo` component; `.seller-*` to the storefront |

## Plan decisions (made while planning)

Lama can veto any of these at plan review. In this plan, "plan decision N" means this list; "spec 2.N" means the spec's decisions; "D decision N" and "E decision N" mean those plans' lists; "decision N" on its own means a row in the decisions log.

1. **A shop's status stays on D's seller profile; its details live on a `sellers` row.** `sellerProfiles` (account → status, storefront slug, applied at) is already the one place D and E read a shop's owner and status from, and keeping it so leaves their code alone. The new `sellers` table holds every shop's public details, keyed by slug, with `ownerId` (null for the 28 sample shops no account owns, which are always open). A pending shop has a `sellers` row with a slug made from its name when the application is sent, so stage G can review it at `/admin/sellers/[slug]`, but its profile's `sellerSlug` stays null until the Hub approves it, as D and E expect ("a shop waiting for approval has none yet"). Stage G's approval sets `sellerSlug` and the status; G adds `declined` and a staff pause with a reason to the profile.
2. **What buyers see.** A listing is *listed* (browse, Just listed, collections, More from, Free & swaps, the storefront, search) when it's available and its shop is approved. It has a *page* when it isn't a draft and its shop is approved or paused; in a paused shop it shows as paused ("Not taking orders right now", no Message), so links people saved still land somewhere. A paused shop keeps its storefront, which then shows "Nothing listed right now"; a pending shop has no storefront, and its drafts have no pages. Featured and the Hub's Verified list draw only from approved shops. Stage G's hidden listings join these rules in `lib/data/catalog.ts`.
3. **One state per listing:** `draft`, `available`, `paused` or `sold` (My listings' four tabs). **Save and publish** makes a new listing or a draft available; on a paused or sold listing the same button says **Save changes** and keeps its state. **Save as draft** always makes it a draft. A listing's `createdAt` is when it was first published, so a draft published later counts as just listed. A sold listing's row offers **Relist** (back to available), so marking sold by mistake can be undone; the spec's row actions don't otherwise allow it.
4. **A shop waiting for approval saves drafts only** (spec 2.7): its listing form has **Save as draft** and a note, and the server turns a forged publish into a draft. Once approved, the seller publishes each draft from its form.
5. **Drafts are complete listings.** A draft passes the same checks as a published listing, so every row in the store is a valid listing and publishing is one click.
6. **Tags.** Functions that return listings are tagged `listings` and `sellers` (a shop's status and badge change its listings); functions that return only sellers are tagged `sellers`. A listing write calls `updateTag("listings")`, a shop write `updateTag("sellers")`, both through `lib/data/tags.ts`; then `refresh()` and a `replace` redirect (E decision 6). D's account deletion and its sample sign-in (when it brings a sample shop back) call them too.
7. **The fixtures stay the seed, and tests keep importing them.** `data/fixtures/shops.ts` turns the fixture listings and sellers into rows; `seedStore()` copies them. The catalog turns them back into exactly the fixture objects (a test checks every one), so the data layer's existing tests pass unchanged except for a `resetSampleStore()` before each, and `collection-limit.test.ts`'s fixture mock still works because the store seeds from the mocked module. `"use cache"` can't run in Vitest, and `cacheTag` throws outside it, so `vitest.setup.ts` stands `next/cache` in with no-ops.
8. **Prerendering and new ids.** `next build` runs each page in a fresh process whose store is seeded from the fixtures, so `generateStaticParams` still prerenders the 38 sample listings and the 29 open sample shops. A new listing's page gets the App Shell on its first request, then the rendered page, which Next keeps until a write expires its tags. **Sample-mode quirk:** pages Next re-renders after a write are kept on disk, but the store lives in memory, so restarting `next start` without rebuilding can show listings the store has forgotten. Rebuild after a restart (`pnpm e2e` always does); Supabase ends it, because the data then outlives the server.
9. **The photo field is one list, saved and new photos together** (spec 2.3, 2.5 and section 4). Every photo row has a hidden `photo` input (a saved photo's address, or `new:<n>` for the n-th file in the file input), a description field ("Describe photo 2"), and **Move earlier**, **Move later**, **Make cover** and **Remove**, all submit buttons named `photoMove`. Without the app's JavaScript, a button posts the form: the server stores any new files, applies the move, keeps everything typed, and comes back to `#…-photos`. With it, `PhotoField` handles the buttons in place, announces the move, and keeps focus on the moved photo. The first photo is the cover. An empty description means "use the title" (spec 2.5), so it follows the title when that changes.
10. **The browser's part** (spec 2.3): each picked photo is drawn to a canvas with its longest side at most 1,600px and re-encoded as WebP at 0.8, which drops every bit of hidden data. Safari can't encode WebP, so when the result isn't WebP it encodes JPEG at 0.8 instead. Even a small photo is re-encoded. The re-encoded files replace the input's files through a `DataTransfer`, so the plain form still posts them, and a photo the browser can't decode is sent as it is for the server to check.
11. **The server's part** (spec 2.3): JPEG, PNG and WebP only, by signature; 5 MB each; 6 per listing; and the metadata stripper on every file, re-encoded or not. The stripper keeps only what the image needs (spec 8.4, amendment 8): an allowlist for each format, with everything else dropped. JPEG: DQT, DHT, DAC, DRI, SOFn, the scans, DNL and EXP; APP0 only for JFIF, APP2 only for `ICC_PROFILE`, APP14 only for Adobe's; an Orientation other than 1 comes back in a minimal APP1. PNG: `IHDR`, `PLTE`, `IDAT`, `IEND`, `tRNS`, `gAMA`, `cHRM`, `sRGB`, `iCCP`, `sBIT`, `cICP`, `mDCV`, `cLLI`, `pHYs`, `bKGD`, `acTL`, `fcTL` and `fdAT`. WebP: `VP8X` (its EXIF and XMP flags cleared), `VP8 `, `VP8L`, `ALPH`, `ICCP`, `ANIM` and `ANMF` (only each frame's image chunks). Nothing after the image's end is kept, and a file with no image is refused. Photos uploaded on a form that hasn't saved yet are kept for a day (12 per account at most), so a failed save doesn't lose them.
12. **No server copies of resized photos** (`images.maximumDiskCacheSize: 0`). Next's image optimizer otherwise keeps each resized photo on disk for as long as the source's cache header says, a year here, so a photo deleted with its listing or account (POPIA) could still be served. With the disk cache off, nothing is kept on the server; browsers still cache what they're sent. The cost is a resize per uncached request, which is fine for a preview; only uploaded photos are optimized (the SVG placeholders never were). Go-live: Supabase Storage's transforms and a purge on delete replace it.
13. **The form's thumbnails come from the server.** The listing form page works out each saved photo's small `/_next/image` address with `getImageProps()` and hands `PhotoField` plain strings, so the photo field draws `<img>` elements and pulls in none of `next/image`'s client code.
14. **Test images are built in code, not committed** (`tests/helpers/image-bytes.ts`). Each JPEG, PNG and WebP case (with and without Exif, XMP, IPTC, text chunks and odd-sized chunks) is a few lines anyone can read in the diff, and there are no binary files to review or keep in step. The PNG helper writes a real, decodable image (zlib from Node), which the end-to-end tests upload.
15. **The wizard** (spec 2.6): the draft holds whatever was typed, and each step's errors sit in D's form state; **Continue** saves and moves on only when the step is right. **Back** is a link that replaces the history entry. Step 3's photo is optional: Continue with no file uses the category's picture, and **Remove photo** takes an uploaded one away. Step 4 shows the pickup points of the campus it was drawn with (a hidden `pointsCampus`); choosing another campus and pressing Continue comes back with that campus's points and asks for them, with no client code. **Send for approval** checks every step again and goes to the first step with a problem.
16. **Shop names and links.** A name is unique on its campus, compared without case, accents or punctuation ("Tech Corner!" matches "tech corner"). The link is made from the name (`crochet-corner`, then `crochet-corner-2`), and while the shop waits for approval it follows the name. Once approved, it changes only when the seller changes it, once (spec 2.11), and the change carries over to the shop's listings, its conversations and reports about it; old links then 404.
17. **Changing the shop's campus** moves its listings to the new campus, and a pickup listing whose point isn't among the shop's new pickup points moves to the first one (or to "Online" when the new campus has none).
18. **The dashboard's numbers** (spec 2.8) cover the last 7 days and say so ("In the last 7 days"). An enquiry is a buyer's first message about a listing (the trust page's words), so a question to the shop isn't one. "Replied within 48 hours" is "3 of 4": enquiries still inside their 48 hours don't count yet (the trust page). Sales count when the buyer confirms, or 7 days after the seller marks done (E's rule, applied when counting; nothing is written). The badge shown is the stored one (stage G recalculates it); its progress line works out the next badge's rules from the last 90 days: "1 more enquiry and 5 more confirmed sales for Trusted."
19. **The owner's links** (spec 2.12). E's `ViewerMessageLink` first asks `ownerLinkFor(target)`: for the owner of the listing's shop it renders **Edit listing** (`/sell/listings/[id]/edit`), and on their own storefront **Edit shop** (`/sell/profile`), with the same look and `prefetch={false}`. A paused or sold listing still shows "See similar items" to everyone; its owner edits it from My listings.
20. **No prefetching into the selling pages from public pages.** Every link this plan adds to a `/sell/…` page has `prefetch={false}`, and `/sell` itself renders no client component and no `Photo` (the header prefetches it on every page).
21. **The account page's Selling row** points an approved or paused shop at `/sell` (the dashboard) rather than the storefront (D decision 16 left it for stage F).
22. **The data export** gains `shop` (its details, status and link) and `listings` (each with its state, fields and photos as their web addresses and descriptions). The photos' addresses are how the student gets their own photos back; they hold no other id. **Deleting the account** removes the shop, its listings, every photo the account uploaded and any wizard draft; conversations keep the listing's title and say it was removed (E).
23. **Ids on the forms are unique per page.** Next keeps earlier pages hidden in the DOM, so a label could find a hidden page's field with the same id. Fields are prefixed `onb-` (wizard), `profile-` (Edit shop), `new-` (new listing) and `edit-<id>-` (editing that listing).
24. **Errors.** Each form's errors use D's `ErrorSummary` and `FieldError`, worded in `lib/selling/errors.ts`. The photo field's errors link to its file input.
25. **End to end** (spec section 6's testing list). Tests that only read, or that write nothing buyers see (a new student's application and drafts, the sample seller's pages, the form's errors), run in the five device projects. Tests that publish, edit, pause, sell or delete listings run in a sixth project, `selling-writes`, which depends on the other five and so runs after them: browse's and the storefront's counts can't move under another test. They use the sample seller (Tech corner) and only ever touch the test's own new listing, found by a unique title. No end-to-end test pauses Tech corner or changes its link; unit tests cover both. There's no test-only route to approve a shop: approval is stage G's.
26. **The listing form's choices** follow the listing model (browse and listing spec 6.1) and FUNCTION-MAP G-7: every field shows, whatever the kind or pay mode, because without JavaScript nothing can hide them; hints say which apply. Price is whole rand from R1 to R100,000 ("R1 200" and "1,200" are fine); **It's free** makes it R0 and needs Cash; services may tick "This is a starting price"; swaps need "What would you swap for?"; a pickup needs one of the shop's pickup points, and a shop on Eduvos Online has no pickup choice. A listing needs no photo: without one it shows its category's picture, as the samples do.
27. **Wording the spec doesn't give** (Lama may change it): the pitch's benefits and steps (`components/sell/SellPitch.tsx`); the waiting page (`ShopWaiting.tsx`); the dashboard's lines and the badge progress (`Dashboard.tsx`, `lib/selling/badges.ts`); every error (`lib/selling/errors.ts`); the notices after each action (`lib/selling/notices.ts`); the hints and empty states in each view; Lerato's sample shop, "Crochet corner" (`data/fixtures/shops.ts`).
28. **Not in this stage:** approving, declining, verifying or staff-pausing shops and hiding listings (stage G), tags on listings (FUNCTION-MAP's G-7 has them; the spec doesn't), view counts, bulk actions, and scheduled listings (spec 7).

## Amendments on review

The controller adds amendments here; they override the tasks below wherever they differ. Amendments 1 to 6 carry stage E's build lessons into this plan (Claude, under decision 54, 2026-10-14).

1. **Every error summary has its own id** (messaging plan amendment 1). `ErrorSummary` takes a required `id`; its heading's id is `${id}-title`. Use the form's field prefix (plan decision 23): `onb-step-<n>-summary`, `profile-summary`, `new-summary`, `edit-<id>-summary`. Each task that adds a form also adds it to `components/forms/summary-ids.test.tsx`, rendered with one error beside the forms already there.
2. **A form on a page that differs only by its query is keyed by that query** (messaging plan amendment 6.1). `/sell/onboarding?step=1` and `?step=2` are one page to Next's router, so a kept step would carry what was typed into the next. Each wizard step's `<form>` has `key={`step-${step}`}`, and the listing form's key names the listing (`new` or its id). A test rerenders the view for another step and expects empty fields.
3. **Field ids are unique per item** (messaging plan amendment 5); plan decision 23 already does this with `edit-<id>-`. The ids that hints and errors join through `aria-describedby`, and the photo rows' controls, follow the same prefix. A test renders two edit forms side by side and checks that every `for`, `href="#…"` and `aria-describedby` resolves inside its own form.
4. **Every link this plan adds has `prefetch={false}`,** calls to action included (messaging plan amendment 6.3), not only links to `/sell/…`.
5. **What stage E built differently from its plan** (read the code, not E's plan):
   - When a conversation's buyer or seller deletes their account, a Done sale goes back to Replied, and a conversation left empty is deleted. The deleted side is named "Deleted account".
   - Field and summary ids on E's pages end with their item.
   - `lib/data/messages.ts`'s `settle` and `saleRow` are async.
   - `robots.ts` has a site-hidden branch first (decision 68); leave it.
   - E's final JS figures, the start of this plan's ledger, at 390px, brotli quality 5: `/` 150,613 B, browse 151,360 B, listing 151,769 B, storefront 151,373 B, request and collection 151,089 B, Saved 151,248 B, every signed-in messaging page 150,613 B. The stop line is 152,305 B.
6. **Two limits of the hosted preview** (https://vossie-pi.vercel.app, decision 68):
   - A request body over 4.5 MB fails there before it reaches the action. With the app's JavaScript, photos are re-encoded small (plan decision 10), so a normal listing fits. Without it, large originals fail. Spec 8.2's go-live item stands.
   - The sample store, uploaded photos included, lives in one server instance's memory, so a photo may not show from another instance.

   Neither changes a task. Task 18 lists both in PROGRESS's open points.
7. **What the recheck found, for later tasks** (Task 1's recheck, 2026-10-14). Nothing changed a quoted line of code; these are the differences to adapt to.
   - **Task 14:**
     - `tests/helpers/viewer-links.tsx` (`signedOutViewerLinks`) stands in for `ViewerLinks` in component tests. It must learn the owner's links, or stay compatible with them.
     - `SellingRow` today links an approved or paused shop to `/sellers/${slug}` with `prefetch={false}`, and a waiting shop to `/sell`. Both become `/sell`, with `prefetch={false}`.
   - **Tasks 12 and 17:**
     - `performance.spec.ts`'s signed-in measure is a loop over `SIGNED_IN_PAGES` rows (`[name, path, accountLabel]`): add rows to it.
     - `cls.spec.ts`'s fourth `PAGES` value is `signIn?: string`, the sample account's button label, not a boolean.
     - Add rows; don't rewrite the tests.
   - **Everywhere:** `ConversationRow` also has `sellerGone` and `lastMessageAt`. The decisions log ends at row 70.
8. **The review of Tasks 1 to 5** (reviewer af56ff3e on Tasks 1 to 3, and the controller's reviewer on Tasks 1 to 4; Claude, under decision 54, 2026-10-14). A fix task runs after Task 5 and before Task 6, test first. These override Tasks 2 and 3 and plan decision 11.
   1. **The stripper keeps an allowlist per format, and drops everything else** (important: a denylist let C2PA, APP11 and other blocks that can hold a location through, and `/photos/[id]` serves the stored bytes for a year).
      - JPEG: keep DQT, DHT, DAC, DRI, SOFn, SOS, DNL, EXP, RSTn and TEM; APP0 only when it starts `JFIF\0` or `JFXX\0`; APP2 only when it starts `ICC_PROFILE\0`; APP14 only when it starts `Adobe`. Drop every other APPn and COM. Refuse (null) the reserved markers F0 to FD.
      - PNG: keep IHDR, PLTE, IDAT, IEND, tRNS, gAMA, cHRM, sRGB, iCCP, sBIT, cICP, mDCV, cLLI, pHYs, bKGD, acTL, fcTL and fdAT. Drop every other chunk (eXIf, zxIf, caBX, the text chunks, tIME and private chunks among them).
      - WebP: keep VP8X, VP8, VP8L, ALPH, ICCP, ANIM and ANMF, and clear VP8X's EXIF (0x08) and XMP (0x04) flags. Drop every other chunk (EXIF, "XMP ", "XMP\0", "exif", C2PA). Inside ANMF, keep only its frame data chunks (VP8, VP8L, ALPH).
      - Tests: a JPEG with APP11 carrying a GPS string, a PNG with `caBX` and a WebP with a `C2PA` chunk carrying one, each stripped of it; and the existing keep-and-drop tests updated to the allowlist.
   2. **Orientation survives** (minor). When a JPEG's Exif held an Orientation other than 1, the stripper writes back a minimal APP1 holding only tag 0x0112 (about 34 bytes, no location), so a portrait photo uploaded without the app's JavaScript doesn't show sideways. Orientation 1 or none: no APP1. Test both.
   3. **Files with no image are refused** (minor): JPEG needs a SOFn and a SOS before EOI; PNG needs IHDR first and at least one IDAT; WebP needs a VP8, VP8L or ANMF chunk. `stripPng` and `stripWebp` check their own signatures. Tests: `FF D8 FF D9`, a PNG signature plus IEND, and `RIFF 04 00 00 00 WEBP` are all refused.
   4. **Four guard tests** (minor): a progressive JPEG with APP1 or COM between scans; a WebP whose RIFF size is smaller than the file with EXIF after it; a lossy VP8 WebP; and eXIf after IEND. The stripper's header comments say what the code does.
   5. **`parsePrice` takes whole rand only** (important: "R250,00", South Africa's decimal comma, became 25000). Accept, after an optional R and spaces: plain digits, or digits with commas or spaces in groups of three (`1,200`, `1 200`, `12,500`), optionally followed by `,00` or `.00`. Anything else is invalid. Tests: "R250,00" → 250, "1,200" → 1200, "R1 200,00" → 1200, "99,99" → null, "1,20" → null.
   6. **Shop names compare in any alphabet** (minor). `nameKey` drops `'` and `’`, removes combining marks (`\p{M}`), keeps letters and digits in any script (`\p{L}\p{N}`) and turns the rest into single spaces. A shop name needs at least one letter or digit (the name check's error is the existing invalid-name one). `slugify` adds `-shop` to a slug shorter than `SLUG_MIN`. Tests: "Zoe's Cafe" and "Zoes Cafe" collide; an all-emoji name is refused; "AB" slugifies to "ab-shop".
   7. **Badge progress floors** (minor): `Math.floor`, not `Math.round`, so 39 of 49 isn't shown as "80%" against an 80% target.
   8. **The price limit's words** (minor): "Keep the price to R100,000 or less.", since exactly R100,000 is allowed.
   9. **Task 5's rename:** `usePhotos` is `attachPhotos` (the React Hooks lint treats `use…` as a hook). Task 7 restores the sample shop (`restoreSampleShop` in `signInAsSample`); until then, a deleted sample seller signs back in without Tech corner.
   10. **Task 6's end-to-end test** also checks that `/photos/…` sends `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`.
   11. The spec's 8.4 and plan decision 11 are reworded to say "keeps only what the image needs" and list the allowlist.
   12. **The stripper can't be made to use far more memory than the file** (from the controller's review; a 5 MB file of top-level `FF D0` grew the heap by 342 MB and blocked for 392 ms, so six such photos could take a small instance down). RSTn and TEM are refused outside a scan's data (the scan loop already passes the real ones); kept bytes are copied into one `new Uint8Array(bytes.length)` with a running offset and returned as `subarray(0, n)`, in all three formats, never one view per segment or chunk. Zero-length segments and chunks are still parsed, but cost nothing extra. Test: a 5 MB file of `FF D0` after SOI is refused, and a file of 100,000 zero-length DQT segments (`FF DB 00 02`) finishes with the output no larger than the input.
   13. **The cache tags are tested** (controller's review). `vitest.setup.ts`'s `cacheTag` stand-in is a `vi.fn`, and `index.test.ts` asserts that every listings-returning function calls `cacheTag("listings", "sellers")` and that `getSeller`, `getSellerSlugs`, `getFeatured` and `getVerifiedSellers` call `cacheTag("sellers")`.
   14. **"Kept out of every list" is tested on every list** (controller's review; spec 8.8). In one test Tech corner is made verified and paused, and it's absent from Featured, Verified, search (by its name), collections, Free & swaps, More from and Related; the same again with a verified shop waiting for approval.
   15. **Each limit is tested at the limit too** (controller's review): a 70-character title, R1 and R100,000, a 1,000-character description, a 200-character swap note and a 600-character about all pass, beside the existing first-past-the-limit refusals.
9. **The review of Tasks 5 to 7** (the controller's reviewer; nothing Critical or Important; Claude, under decision 54, 2026-10-15). A fix task runs after Task 9 and before Task 10, test first.
   1. **Conversations whose seller has gone don't count.** `shopStats` (`lib/data/shop-stats.ts`) skips rows with `sellerGone`, and `moveShop` (`lib/data/shops.ts`) leaves them where they are. Otherwise a restored sample shop, or a new shop that reuses a deleted shop's link, inherits enquiries and sales its owner can't open. Test both.
   2. **A first publish is a first publish.** `ListingRow` gains `firstPublishedAt: string | null` (seeded listings: their `createdAt`; drafts: null). `saveOwnListing`'s `firstPublish` is `firstPublishedAt === null`, which sets it and `createdAt`; republishing a listing that was saved back as a draft keeps both, so it isn't "just listed" again. Stages H and I hang their follower and saved-search notifications on this same `firstPublish`, so it must not fire twice. Test: publish, save as draft, publish again: `createdAt` and `firstPublishedAt` unchanged, and `firstPublish` false the second time. `STORE_VERSION` goes up by one.
   3. **The sample shop always comes back whole.** When `restoreSampleShop` finds its seed link taken by another shop, it restores Tech corner under `freeSlug(…)` and updates the restored profile's `sellerSlug` to match, so the profile never claims another student's shop. `sendForApproval` handles `createShop` returning null with the existing "something went wrong" notice instead of landing on `/sell` with no shop. Test both.
   4. **Photos are cached on the device only.** `/photos/[id]` sends `private, max-age=31536000, immutable`: a phone keeps the bytes (mobile data), and no shared cache or CDN keeps a photo after its listing is deleted. Whether Vercel's own image optimiser keeps resized copies of a deleted photo is a go-live check (Task 18 lists it in PROGRESS's open points with amendment 6).
   5. **A waiting photo expires when it's asked for, too.** `/photos/[id]` returns 404 for a photo with no use (waiting on a form) that is more than a day old, read after `connection()`, so the limit doesn't wait for the next upload.
   6. **The export includes the wizard.** `exportShop` (or the account export) adds `onboardingDraft`: the draft's answers and its photo's address, no ids, when the account has one.
   7. **Two tag tests.** `account-actions.test.ts` mocks `next/cache` with `nextCache(request)` and asserts `updatedTags` is `["sellers"]` after the sample seller deletes their account (spec 2.13: gone straight away); `sign-in-actions.test.ts` asserts the same after the sample seller's button restores Tech corner.
   8. **Task 16 fetches a real photo.** Its selling e2e uploads a photo, then requests its `/photos/…` address and checks the 200 has an `image/*` type, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and the `private` cache header, completing amendment 8 item 10.
   9. **Task 9's departures stand:** `/sell` reads the viewer with `getViewer().catch(() => null)` because the pitch is public; the shell test expects the new h1; `components/ComingSoon.tsx` is unused now and stays for Task 18 to list.
10. **The review of Tasks 8 to 10, and Task 11's notes** (the controller's reviewer; nothing Critical; Claude, under decision 54, 2026-10-15). A fix task runs after Task 11 and before Task 12, test first.
   1. **A saved listing never points at a photo that's gone** (important, rare). In `saveListingForm` and the photo buttons (`lib/selling/listing-actions.ts`), the photos a form may use are worked out after `storePhotos` has tidied (re-read `photoSrcsOf`, or filter the order to photos that still exist), and `photoSrcsOf` takes `now` and skips a waiting photo a day old. Test: a waiting photo more than a day old is dropped from a listing saved the next day, and the listing keeps its other photos.
   2. **Form state keeps only bounded values.** `typedValues` passes every field through `keepTyped`, or keeps a choice field (`kind`, `category`, `condition`, `free`, `from`, `payMode`, `handover`, `pickup`) only when it's one of its known values. Test: a 1 MB `kind` is stored as nothing, or at most 4,000 characters.
   3. **Remove makes room in the same post.** Without JavaScript, a Remove pressed with a new file chosen applies the removal before `room` is worked out, so a listing with 6 photos takes the new one. Test it.
   4. **An Online student resumes at the review.** `resumeStep` (`components/sell/SellHome.tsx`) returns step 3 only when the draft's campus has pickup points to choose; otherwise it's 5. Test with `campusSlug: "online"`.
   5. **Step 5 starts clean.** A successful Continue on step 4 clears `onboardingForm(5)`, so an error left by an earlier Send for approval doesn't greet the student before they press anything. Test it.
   6. **Notices show once.** The dashboard's and My listings' notices ("Paused. …", "Your shop is paused…") are taken when shown (read and cleared, as E's thread takes "Sent."), not left for their 10 minutes. Test: a second visit shows no notice.
   7. **Tests the review asked for:** `/sell`'s links all have `prefetch={false}` (Dashboard, the pitch and the waiting page, with the wizard test's `next/link` stand-in); `RadioGroup` with an error has no `aria-invalid` on any radio and the group's description includes the error; a save can't use a photo of another of the seller's own listings (`listing:l-002`); Delete listing expires the listings tag (`updatedTags`).
   8. **For Task 17 (layout shift):** `SellFormSkeleton` is 32rem; the delete page's content is about 14rem, so the footer moves about 240px when it arrives at 390px. Task 17 sizes the delete page's skeleton to its content.
   9. **Kept as built:** an unknown delete id with the app's JavaScript blocked shows the skeleton, not the 404 (`notFound()` after streaming starts, status 200), as every request-time page here does; the go-live proxy check covers status codes. Task 11's underline on links inside the selling column's notices stays.

## Store design

The four tables, how they fit D's and E's store, and what stage G adds:

- **`sellers`** (by slug): `SellerRow`, every shop's public details (name, tagline, category, campus, about, photo, Verified, badge, selling since), its owner account (or null), the pickup points it uses, whether its link has been changed, and when it was made and last changed. Seeded from `data/fixtures/sellers.ts` (owners from `sampleShopOwners`, pickup points from the campus) plus Lerato's waiting shop. **Status is not here:** it's on the owner's `sellerProfiles` row (plan decision 1); a shop with no owner is approved. Stage G adds mentor assignment and the Featured history in tables of its own.
- **`listings`** (by id): `ListingRow`, the listing model's fields plus its shop's slug, its `state` (plan decision 3), its campus (the shop's), its photos in order (`ImageRef`s whose empty `alt` means "the title"), and `createdAt` and `updatedAt`. Seeded from `data/fixtures/listings.ts`. Stage G adds a hidden flag and reason.
- **`photos`** (by id, `ph-<uuid>`): the uploader, what it's used for (`listing:<id>`, `shop`, `draft`, or null while it waits on a form), the type and the stripped bytes. Not seeded: the samples use placeholder art. Only `/photos/[id]` hands the bytes out.
- **`onboardingDrafts`** (by account id): the wizard's answers so far (spec 2.2).
- **Seeds:** `data/fixtures/shops.ts` (types only from `lib`; it reads the other fixtures), copied by `seedStore()`. `STORE_VERSION` goes up by one.
- **Who touches them:** `lib/data/catalog.ts` (reads, for buyers), `lib/data/shops.ts`, `lib/data/own-listings.ts`, `lib/data/photos.ts` and `lib/data/shop-stats.ts`. `shops.ts` also rewrites a shop's slug in E's `conversations` and `reports` (plan decision 16), the only place selling writes to E's tables. `lib/data/accounts.ts` imports `forgetShopOf`, `exportShop` and `restoreSampleShop`; none of the selling modules imports `accounts.ts`, so there's no cycle.

## The JS ledger

- **Limit:** 153,600 B (brotli, quality 5) on a first visit at 390px, for each page.
- **Stop rule (spec 5):** if any page passes **the stop line Lama last accepted (152,305 B when this plan was written; use the newer figure from "Recheck" item 2 if there is one)**, stop after that task and report the figures. Don't cut features to fit; that's Lama's decision. **The listing form's two pages count as pages:** they stay under the stop line too.
- **Where bytes move:**
  - Tasks 1 to 3 and 5 to 8 add no client code.
  - **Task 4 changes what every public page reads** (the data layer moves into the store). No client code changes, but it's the biggest change to every page's server side, so Task 4 measures every page.
  - **Task 9 replaces `/sell`,** which the header, menu, footer and homepage prefetch on every page. It must add no client component; Task 9 measures every page.
  - Tasks 10, 11 and 13 add selling pages with no client code beyond the shell.
  - **Task 12 adds `PhotoField`,** the only new client component, on `/sell/listings/new` and `/sell/listings/[id]/edit` only. Task 12 measures both pages, signed in as the sample seller.
  - **Task 14 changes the listing page and the storefront** (Server Components only: the owner check inside E's streamed link). Task 14 measures every page.
- **Recording.** `tests/e2e/performance.spec.ts` prints each page's `scriptBrotli`. Tasks 4, 9, 12, 14 and 18 run it at 390px and put the figures in their commit messages; Task 9 adds `/sell` to it, and Task 12 adds the two form pages, measured signed in as the sample seller.

The highest page is the listing page, 536 B under the stop line and 1,831 B under the limit.

## Conventions for every task

- **Paths and commands.** Paths are relative to `/Users/LamaNkuna/Projects/Vossie`, and commands run from there.
- **Code style.** TypeScript, double quotes, semicolons, 2-space indent, lines up to about 120 characters, and comments in plain English. There's no formatter: match the file you're in.
- **Server or client.** Server Components unless a file starts with `"use client"`. This plan adds **one** client component, `components/sell/PhotoField.tsx` (Task 12). Plain `lib/` modules have no directive. `lib/selling/shop-actions.ts` and `lib/selling/listing-actions.ts` start with `"use server"`: everything they export must be an async function (types are fine; constants are not).
- **Next APIs.** Before using a Next API this plan names, read its page in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/` (AGENTS.md). This plan uses:
  - `cacheTag` and `updateTag`: `03-api-reference/04-functions/cacheTag.md`, `updateTag.md`, `01-getting-started/09-revalidating.md`, `02-guides/how-revalidation-works.md`;
  - prerendering with `generateStaticParams` and new ids: `02-guides/incremental-static-regeneration-cache-components.md`, `03-api-reference/04-functions/generate-static-params.md`, `03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/dynamic-routes.md`;
  - Server Actions, forms, `refresh()`, `redirect()`/`RedirectType`, `notFound()`: `02-guides/server-actions.md`, `02-guides/forms.md`, `03-api-reference/04-functions/refresh.md`, `redirect.md`, `not-found.md`, `03-api-reference/01-directives/use-server.md`;
  - the body limit: `03-api-reference/05-config/01-next-config-js/serverActions.md`;
  - a GET route handler: `03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/route.md`, `01-getting-started/15-route-handlers.md` ("With Cache Components");
  - images: `03-api-reference/02-components/image.md` (`getImageProps`, `maximumDiskCacheSize`, `minimumCacheTTL`);
  - the session behind `<Suspense>`: `02-guides/authentication-with-cache-components.md`;
  - kept pages: `02-guides/preserving-ui-state.md`.

  The first task that uses one says so in its first step.
- **Breakpoints.**
  - `wide:` is tablet and up; `desk:` is desktop. Never use `md:`, `lg:` or `sm:`.
  - In CSS, write `@media (min-width: 48rem) and (min-height: 30rem)` and `@media (min-width: 64rem) and (min-height: 30rem)`. Landscape phones are `@media (orientation: landscape) and (max-height: 29.99rem)`.
- **Page padding.** `.page` and the page's own class (`.sell-page`) sit on one element, so `.sell-page`'s padding *replaces* `.page`'s gutter. Write the whole content edge: 16px on phones, 40px from the tablet step.
- **Effects.** The one client component sets no state in an effect and touches refs only in handlers. It uses no effect at all.
- **Headings.** One H1 per page. Sections are `<section aria-labelledby="…">` with one H2, visible or `sr-only`. Ids are unique across pages (`sell-…`, `onb-…`, `profile-…`, `new-…`, `edit-<id>-…`), because Next keeps earlier pages hidden in the DOM (plan decision 23).
- **Request-time parts.** Anything that reads cookies, headers, `params`, `searchParams` or the clock sits inside `<Suspense>`. Clock reads come after `await connection()` (decision 26). No `Date.now()`, `new Date()`, `Math.random()` or `crypto.random*` while prerendering: new ids are made only in Server Actions. There's no `try`/`catch` round JSX: the React Compiler lint rejects it.
- **Data functions** take `now` from their caller, never read the clock, and hand out copies (D's "Store design"). A photo's bytes are shared, never copied: nothing changes them after they're stored.
- **Server Actions.** Read every field with `field(formData, name)` (a missing field or a file is `""`). Never take identity or ownership from a field: the account comes from `requireAccount()`, the shop from `requireShopOwner()`, and every listing id from a field is looked up among that shop's listings. Call `redirect()` outside any `try`. Every action ends with `done(path)` (`refresh()`, then a `replace` redirect); one that changed what buyers see calls `listingsChanged()` or `sellersChanged()` first.
- **Forms.** Plain `<form action={serverAction} noValidate>`: `noValidate` so the server's messages show rather than the browser's bubbles; fields keep `required` where they're required, so it's announced. Every field has a `<label>` (a `<legend>` for radio groups and the pickup points); hints and errors join `aria-describedby`; an invalid field has `aria-invalid="true"`. Errors use D's `ErrorSummary` and `FieldError`.
- **User text.** Shop names, taglines, about texts, titles, descriptions, swap terms and photo descriptions are React text, never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`.
- **Async components in tests.** jsdom can't render an async component inside a tree. Render `await Thing(props)` directly, or stand the async part in with `vi.mock`.
- **Server code in tests.** Use D's `tests/helpers/next-request.ts` for `next/headers`, `next/navigation`, `next/server` and `next/cache`; call `resetSampleStore()` and `resetRequest(request)` in `beforeEach`. Fake only the date: `vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ["Date"] })`, then `vi.setSystemTime(…)`.
- **JSX text.** The `react/no-unescaped-entities` rule is on: write `&apos;` for an apostrophe inside JSX text (a string in an attribute, a prop or `{"…"}` is fine). JSX drops the space at a line break next to a tag, so end a text line with `{" "}` when the next line starts with a tag.
- **Links.** Pages on this site use `Link` from `next/link`. Every link this plan adds has `prefetch={false}` (plan decision 20). A plain `<a href>` is for a `#` jump and other websites.
- **Accessible names.** Name computation trims each element's own text, so put spaces outside `sr-only` spans. Icons from `components/icons.tsx` are `aria-hidden`. A button or link that acts on one listing names it: "Pause <span className="sr-only">{title}</span>".
- **Contrast.**
  - Sky never carries text, icons or thin borders on white. Links on white are sky-dark; on panel and pale they're navy.
  - Danger red is 6.6:1 on white, and white on it is 6.6:1.
- **Motion.** Animate only `transform`/`opacity`. Hover effects only under `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`. This plan adds no animation.
- **Tap targets.** At least 44 × 44px, with 8px between targets. Links inside a paragraph are exempt (WCAG 2.5.8).
- **Class names.** New classes start with `sell-`. Before adding one, check it isn't taken: `grep -n "\.<name>\b" app/globals.css`.
- **CSS placement.**
  - New CSS goes at the end of `app/globals.css`, inside `@layer components`, under a banner: `/* --- Selling: <name> (selling plan, Task N, spec x) --- */`.
  - Never put `!important` on a transition or animation property: the motion off-switches rely on it.
- **Hidden pages in tests.** In e2e tests, find things with `getByRole`/`getByLabel` and `.filter({ visible: true })`; a CSS locator also counts the kept copies.
- **TDD.** Write the test, watch it fail, write the code, watch it pass.
- **Handing over.** Implementers don't run `git add` or `git commit`. The last step of each task lists the files and the commit message. The controller then:
  1. runs `git status --short` on its own, and checks that only the listed files changed (and `.claude/launch.json`, which is never staged);
  2. runs `git add` with the listed files;
  3. runs `git commit` with the given message, ending with a blank line and `Co-Authored-By: <model> <noreply@anthropic.com>`.
  - One git command per tool call.
- **Looking at it.** Check visually on a production build (`pnpm build`, then `pnpm start --port 3201`) at 390 × 844, 768 × 1024 and 1280 × 800, using a throwaway Playwright script in your scratchpad. Stop the server afterwards (it's yours, not an install or build).
- **Measuring JS.** `pnpm e2e tests/e2e/performance.spec.ts --project=phone-390` builds, starts its own server on 3200, and prints one line per page, such as `listing page: {"script":…,"scriptBrotli":…}`. Record `scriptBrotli`.

```ts

// --- Selling (selling plan, Task 1) -------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/** A listing as its seller sees it (selling spec 2.9, plan decision 3): a draft, or available, paused or sold. */
export type ListingState = "draft" | Availability;

/** My listings' tabs (selling spec 2.9). */
export type ListingsTab = "available" | "paused" | "sold" | "drafts";

/** The photo files the server takes (selling spec 2.3), by their real signature. */
export type PhotoType = "image/jpeg" | "image/png" | "image/webp";

/** Enquiries and sales over a stretch of time, from a shop's conversations (selling spec 2.8, plan decision 18). */
export type EnquiryCounts = {
  /** Buyers' first messages about a listing. */
  enquiries: number;
  /** Enquiries answered within 48 hours, or older than 48 hours: the ones the reply rate counts. */
  due: number;
  /** Enquiries the shop first answered within 48 hours. */
  repliedInTime: number;
  /** Sales the buyer confirmed, or that confirmed themselves 7 days after the seller marked them done. */
  salesConfirmed: number;
};

/** The dashboard's numbers (selling spec 2.8). */
export type ShopStats = {
  /** The last 7 days. */
  week: EnquiryCounts;
  /** Enquiries over the last 90 days and sales over the shop's whole life: the trust page's badge rules. */
  record: EnquiryCounts;
};
```

```ts

// --- Selling (selling plan, Task 1; "Store design") -------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * A shop (selling spec 2.1 and 2.6), keyed by its slug. Its status lives on its owner's seller profile (plan decision
 * 1); a shop with no owner is a sample shop and is always open.
 */
export type SellerRow = {
  slug: string;
  /** The account that owns it, or null for a sample shop no account owns. */
  ownerId: string | null;
  /** 2 to 60 characters, unique on its campus (selling spec 2.6). */
  name: string;
  /** Up to 80 characters; "" for none. */
  tagline: string;
  categorySlug: string;
  campusSlug: string;
  verified: boolean;
  badge: TrustBadge | null;
  /** Up to 600 characters; line breaks kept. */
  about: string;
  /** YYYY-MM: the month the shop started. */
  since: string;
  /** The shop's photo, or null for its category's picture (selling spec 2.6, step 3). */
  image: ImageRef | null;
  /** The approved pickup points on its campus that the shop uses (selling spec 2.6, step 4). */
  pickupPointIds: string[];
  /** Whether the seller has changed the link (selling spec 2.11: once). */
  slugChanged: boolean;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
};

/** A listing (selling spec 2.10; browse and listing spec 6.1), keyed by id. */
export type ListingRow = {
  id: string;
  /** The shop's slug. */
  sellerSlug: string;
  /** Plan decision 3. */
  state: ListingState;
  kind: ListingKind;
  title: string;
  categorySlug: string;
  /** Items only; null for services. */
  condition: Condition | null;
  /** Rand: 0 when free, null when swap-only. */
  priceZar: number | null;
  priceIsFrom: boolean;
  payMode: PayMode;
  /** Set exactly when it takes swaps. */
  swapFor: string | null;
  description: string;
  handover: Handover;
  /** One of the shop's pickup points when the handover is a pickup; otherwise null. */
  pickupPointId: string | null;
  /** The shop's campus. */
  campusSlug: string;
  /** In order: the first is the cover. An empty alt means "the title" (selling spec 2.5). */
  photos: ImageRef[];
  /** When it was first published; while it's still a draft, when it was made (plan decision 3). */
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
};

/** An uploaded photo (selling spec 2.4), keyed by its id: "ph-" and a UUID. */
export type PhotoRow = {
  id: string;
  /** The account that uploaded it. */
  ownerId: string;
  /** "listing:<id>", "shop" or "draft" once used; null while it waits on a form that hasn't saved (plan decision 11). */
  use: string | null;
  type: PhotoType;
  /** The file with its metadata stripped. Never changed once stored. */
  bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>;
  createdAt: string;
};

/** The onboarding wizard's answers so far (selling spec 2.2 and 2.6), keyed by the account's id. */
export type OnboardingDraftRow = {
  accountId: string;
  name: string;
  tagline: string;
  categorySlug: string;
  about: string;
  /** The shop photo's address ("/photos/ph-…"), or null for the category's picture. */
  photoSrc: string | null;
  campusSlug: string;
  pickupPointIds: string[];
  updatedAt: string;
};
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the sample shops' test**

Create `data/fixtures/shops.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { sampleAccounts } from "./accounts";
import { campuses } from "./campuses";
import { categories } from "./categories";
import { listings } from "./listings";
import { pickupPoints } from "./pickup-points";
import { sampleListingRows, sampleSellerRows, sampleShopOwners } from "./shops";

const pointsById = new Map(pickupPoints.map((point) => [point.id, point]));

describe("the sample shops (selling plan, Task 1)", () => {
  it("has every sample seller, then Lerato's waiting shop, with unique slugs", () => {
    const slugs = sampleSellerRows.map((row) => row.slug);
    expect(new Set(slugs).size).toBe(slugs.length);
    expect(slugs).toHaveLength(30);
    expect(slugs.at(-1)).toBe("crochet-corner");
  });

  it("gives each owned shop to the sample account whose seller profile says so (plan decision 1)", () => {
    const profiles = new Map(
      sampleAccounts.flatMap(({ seller }) => (seller ? [[seller.accountId, seller] as const] : [])),
    );
    for (const [slug, accountId] of sampleShopOwners) {
      const row = sampleSellerRows.find((item) => item.slug === slug);
      expect(row?.ownerId, slug).toBe(accountId);
      const profile = profiles.get(accountId);
      expect(profile, slug).toBeDefined();
      // A waiting shop has a slug, but no storefront yet.
      expect(profile!.sellerSlug, slug).toBe(profile!.status === "pending" ? null : slug);
      const owner = sampleAccounts.find(({ account }) => account.id === accountId)!;
      expect(row!.campusSlug, slug).toBe(owner.account.campusSlug);
    }
    const owned = sampleSellerRows.filter((row) => row.ownerId !== null).map((row) => row.slug);
    expect(owned.sort()).toEqual(["crochet-corner", "tech-corner"]);
  });

  it("puts every shop on a real campus and category, using only its own campus's pickup spots", () => {
    const campusSlugs = new Set(campuses.map((campus) => campus.slug));
    const categorySlugs = new Set(categories.map((category) => category.slug));
    for (const row of sampleSellerRows) {
      expect(campusSlugs.has(row.campusSlug), row.slug).toBe(true);
      expect(categorySlugs.has(row.categorySlug), row.slug).toBe(true);
      for (const id of row.pickupPointIds) expect(pointsById.get(id)?.campusSlug, row.slug).toBe(row.campusSlug);
    }
    expect(sampleSellerRows.find((row) => row.slug === "tech-corner")?.pickupPointIds).toEqual([
      "pp-midrand-library",
      "pp-midrand-reception",
    ]);
    // Eduvos Online has no pickup points.
    expect(sampleSellerRows.find((row) => row.slug === "cv-clinic")?.pickupPointIds).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("turns every sample listing into a row with its state and its photos in order", () => {
    expect(sampleListingRows.map((row) => row.id)).toEqual(listings.map((listing) => listing.id));
    const casio = sampleListingRows.find((row) => row.id === "l-001")!;
    expect(casio).toMatchObject({ sellerSlug: "tech-corner", state: "available", campusSlug: "midrand" });
    // The cover's alt was the title, so it's kept empty: "the title" (selling spec 2.5).
    expect(casio.photos).toEqual([
      { src: "/placeholders/listing/tech.svg", alt: "" },
      { src: "/placeholders/photo/2.svg", alt: "The back of the calculator, with the battery cover" },
      { src: "/placeholders/photo/3.svg", alt: "The slide-on cover" },
    ]);
    expect(sampleListingRows.find((row) => row.id === "l-016")?.state).toBe("paused");
    expect(sampleListingRows.find((row) => row.id === "l-026")?.state).toBe("sold");
  });
});
```

```ts
import type { ListingRow, SellerRow } from "@/lib/data/rows";
import { listings } from "./listings";
import { pickupPoints } from "./pickup-points";
import { sellers } from "./sellers";

// The sample store's shops and listings (selling plan, Task 1; plan decision 7): the listing and seller fixtures as
// rows, plus Lerato's shop, which is waiting for the Hub (selling spec 2.7). Fixtures import nothing from lib at run
// time; these read the other fixtures. Lerato's shop is sample wording for Lama to change.

/** Which sample shops a sample account owns (data/fixtures/accounts.ts). No account owns the other sample shops. */
export const sampleShopOwners: ReadonlyMap<string, string> = new Map([
  ["tech-corner", "acc-seller"],
  ["crochet-corner", "acc-pending"],
]);

/** A campus's pickup spots. Eduvos Online has none. */
const spotsAt = (campusSlug: string) =>
  pickupPoints.filter((point) => point.campusSlug === campusSlug).map((point) => point.id);

/** Lerato's shop (acc-pending): sent for approval on 10 October and still waiting (selling spec 2.7). */
const crochetCorner: SellerRow = {
  slug: "crochet-corner",
  ownerId: "acc-pending",
  name: "Crochet corner",
  tagline: "Beanies, bags and bucket hats, made by hand.",
  categorySlug: "fashion-thrift",
  campusSlug: "pretoria",
  verified: false,
  badge: "new-seller",
  about:
    "I crochet between lectures and sell what I make.\n" +
    "Tell me your colours and I'll make you a beanie or a bag to order.",
  since: "2026-10",
  image: null,
  pickupPointIds: ["pp-pretoria-library"],
  slugChanged: false,
  createdAt: "2026-10-10T12:20:00.000Z",
  updatedAt: "2026-10-10T12:20:00.000Z",
};

export const sampleSellerRows: readonly SellerRow[] = [
  ...sellers.map(
    (seller): SellerRow => ({
      slug: seller.slug,
      ownerId: sampleShopOwners.get(seller.slug) ?? null,
      name: seller.name,
      tagline: seller.tagline,
      categorySlug: seller.categorySlug,
      campusSlug: seller.campusSlug,
      verified: seller.verified,
      badge: seller.badge,
      about: seller.about,
      since: seller.since,
      image: { ...seller.image },
      pickupPointIds: spotsAt(seller.campusSlug),
      slugChanged: false,
      createdAt: `${seller.since}-01T08:00:00.000Z`,
      updatedAt: `${seller.since}-01T08:00:00.000Z`,
    }),
  ),
  crochetCorner,
];

export const sampleListingRows: readonly ListingRow[] = listings.map(
  (listing): ListingRow => ({
    id: listing.id,
    sellerSlug: listing.sellerSlug,
    state: listing.availability,
    kind: listing.kind,
    title: listing.title,
    categorySlug: listing.categorySlug,
    condition: listing.condition,
    priceZar: listing.priceZar,
    priceIsFrom: listing.priceIsFrom,
    payMode: listing.payMode,
    swapFor: listing.swapFor,
    description: listing.description,
    handover: listing.handover,
    pickupPointId: listing.pickupPointId,
    campusSlug: listing.campusSlug,
    // A cover whose alt is the title keeps "" ("the title", selling spec 2.5), so it follows a new title.
    photos: [
      { src: listing.image.src, alt: listing.image.alt === listing.title ? "" : listing.image.alt },
      ...listing.morePhotos.map((photo) => ({ ...photo })),
    ],
    createdAt: listing.createdAt,
    updatedAt: listing.createdAt,
  }),
);
```

### Task 2: Photo files: the limits, the signature check and the metadata stripper

The server's half of spec 2.3 (plan decisions 11 and 14), as pure functions with no Next APIs: what the server takes, how it tells a real JPEG, PNG or WebP from anything else, and how it strips a photo's hidden data without decoding it. The test images are built in code.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the test images**

Create `tests/helpers/image-bytes.ts`:

```ts
import { crc32, deflateSync } from "node:zlib";

/**
 * Tiny photos built byte by byte, for the signature check, the metadata stripper and the end-to-end uploads (selling
 * plan decision 14). Every piece of metadata they carry includes GPS, a stand-in location that a stripped file must
 * not contain. The PNG is a real image any decoder reads; the JPEG and WebP have the real structure around stand-in
 * image data, which is all the stripper reads. Node only (zlib): unit tests and Playwright, never the browser.
 */
export const GPS = "GPS 25.7479S 28.2293E";

const EMPTY = new Uint8Array(0);

/** Text as bytes, one per character. ASCII only. */
export function ascii(text: string): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> {
  return Uint8Array.from(text, (character) => character.charCodeAt(0));
}

export function concatBytes(...parts: readonly Uint8Array[]): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> {
  const out = new Uint8Array(parts.reduce((total, part) => total + part.length, 0));
  let at = 0;
  for (const part of parts) {
    out.set(part, at);
    at += part.length;
  }
  return out;
}

/** Whether `text` appears anywhere in `bytes`. */
export function containsText(bytes: Uint8Array, text: string): boolean {
  return Buffer.from(bytes).includes(text, 0, "latin1");
}

const u16be = (n: number) => [(n >>> 8) & 0xff, n & 0xff];
const u32be = (n: number) => [(n >>> 24) & 0xff, (n >>> 16) & 0xff, (n >>> 8) & 0xff, n & 0xff];
const u32le = (n: number) => [n & 0xff, (n >>> 8) & 0xff, (n >>> 16) & 0xff, (n >>> 24) & 0xff];

// --- JPEG ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/** FF, the marker, a length that counts itself, then the payload. */
function segment(marker: number, payload: Uint8Array): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> {
  return concatBytes(Uint8Array.of(0xff, marker, ...u16be(payload.length + 2)), payload);
}

export type JpegOptions = {
  exif?: boolean;
  xmp?: boolean;
  /** Photoshop's APP13, where IPTC keeps a place. */
  iptc?: boolean;
  comment?: boolean;
  /** Extra FF bytes before a marker, which the format allows. */
  fill?: boolean;
  /** Data after the end of the image, as some phones append (a motion photo's video). */
  trailer?: boolean;
};

/**
 * A baseline JPEG's structure: SOI, JFIF, the metadata asked for, a colour profile, the tables, a frame, one scan
 * whose data has a stuffed FF 00 and a restart marker, and EOI.
 */
export function jpegBytes(options: JpegOptions = {}): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> {
  return concatBytes(
    Uint8Array.of(0xff, 0xd8),
    segment(0xe0, concatBytes(ascii("JFIF\0"), Uint8Array.of(1, 2, 0, 0, 1, 0, 1, 0, 0))),
    options.exif ? segment(0xe1, concatBytes(ascii("Exif\0\0MM\0*"), ascii(GPS))) : EMPTY,
    options.xmp
      ? segment(0xe1, concatBytes(ascii("http://ns.adobe.com/xap/1.0/\0"), ascii(`<x:xmpmeta>${GPS}</x:xmpmeta>`)))
      : EMPTY,
    options.iptc ? segment(0xed, concatBytes(ascii("Photoshop 3.0"), Uint8Array.of(0), ascii(`8BIM ${GPS}`))) : EMPTY,
    options.comment ? segment(0xfe, ascii(`Taken at ${GPS}`)) : EMPTY,
    segment(0xe2, concatBytes(ascii("ICC_PROFILE\0"), Uint8Array.of(1, 1, 0, 0))),
    options.fill ? Uint8Array.of(0xff, 0xff) : EMPTY,
    segment(0xdb, new Uint8Array(65)),
    segment(0xc0, Uint8Array.of(8, 0, 1, 0, 1, 1, 1, 0x11, 0)),
    segment(0xc4, new Uint8Array(20)),
    segment(0xda, Uint8Array.of(1, 1, 0, 0, 0x3f, 0)),
    Uint8Array.of(0x12, 0xff, 0x00, 0x34, 0xff, 0xd0, 0x56),
    Uint8Array.of(0xff, 0xd9),
    options.trailer ? ascii(`ftypmp42 ${GPS}`) : EMPTY,
  );
}

// --- PNG ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

function pngChunk(type: string, data: Uint8Array): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> {
  const body = concatBytes(ascii(type), data);
  return concatBytes(Uint8Array.of(...u32be(data.length)), body, Uint8Array.of(...u32be(crc32(body))));
}

export type PngOptions = {
  width?: number;
  height?: number;
  exif?: boolean;
  /** A tEXt comment. */
  text?: boolean;
  /** A compressed zTXt comment. */
  ztxt?: boolean;
  /** XMP, which PNG keeps in an iTXt chunk. */
  xmp?: boolean;
  /** Data after IEND. */
  trailer?: boolean;
};

/** A real 8-bit RGB PNG, sky blue all over, with the metadata asked for before its image data, and a pHYs chunk. */
export function pngBytes({ width = 8, height = 8, ...options }: PngOptions = {}): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> {
  // Each row starts with filter type 0 (none), then three bytes a pixel.
  const row = [0, ...Array.from({ length: width }, () => [0x29, 0xab, 0xe2]).flat()];
  const pixels = deflateSync(Uint8Array.from(Array.from({ length: height }, () => row).flat()));
  return concatBytes(
    Uint8Array.of(0x89, 0x50, 0x4e, 0x47, 0x0d, 0x0a, 0x1a, 0x0a),
    pngChunk("IHDR", Uint8Array.of(...u32be(width), ...u32be(height), 8, 2, 0, 0, 0)),
    options.exif ? pngChunk("eXIf", concatBytes(ascii("MM\0*"), ascii(GPS))) : EMPTY,
    options.text ? pngChunk("tEXt", concatBytes(ascii("Comment\0"), ascii(GPS))) : EMPTY,
    options.ztxt ? pngChunk("zTXt", concatBytes(ascii("Comment\0\0"), deflateSync(ascii(GPS)))) : EMPTY,
    options.xmp
      ? pngChunk("iTXt", concatBytes(ascii("XML:com.adobe.xmp\0\0\0\0\0"), ascii(`<x:xmpmeta>${GPS}</x:xmpmeta>`)))
      : EMPTY,
    pngChunk("pHYs", Uint8Array.of(0, 0, 0x0b, 0x13, 0, 0, 0x0b, 0x13, 1)),
    pngChunk("IDAT", pixels),
    pngChunk("IEND", EMPTY),
    options.trailer ? ascii(GPS) : EMPTY,
  );
}

// --- WebP ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/** A RIFF chunk: its code, its little-endian size, the data, and a padding byte when the size is odd. */
function riffChunk(code: string, data: Uint8Array): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> {
  return concatBytes(ascii(code), Uint8Array.of(...u32le(data.length)), data, data.length % 2 ? Uint8Array.of(0) : EMPTY);
}

export type WebpOptions = {
  exif?: boolean;
  xmp?: boolean;
  /** A VP8X header without metadata, as a WebP with transparency has. */
  extended?: boolean;
  /** Data after the RIFF. */
  trailer?: boolean;
};

/**
 * A WebP's structure: RIFF, a VP8X header with its flags (alpha, and EXIF and XMP when they're there), a stand-in
 * VP8L image 13 bytes long (so a padding byte follows it), then the metadata asked for.
 */
export function webpBytes(options: WebpOptions = {}): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> {
  const header = options.exif || options.xmp || options.extended;
  // VP8X's first byte holds the flags: alpha 0x10, EXIF 0x08, XMP 0x04. Then 3 reserved bytes, then the canvas size.
  const flags = 0x10 | (options.exif ? 0x08 : 0) | (options.xmp ? 0x04 : 0);
  const body = concatBytes(
    ascii("WEBP"),
    header ? riffChunk("VP8X", Uint8Array.of(flags, 0, 0, 0, 7, 0, 0, 7, 0, 0)) : EMPTY,
    riffChunk("VP8L", Uint8Array.of(0x2f, 7, 0xc0, 0x01, 0x10, 0x8d, 0x24, 0x49, 0x12, 0x20, 0x80, 0x88, 0x08)),
    options.exif ? riffChunk("EXIF", concatBytes(ascii("MM\0*"), ascii(GPS))) : EMPTY,
    options.xmp ? riffChunk("XMP ", ascii(`<x:xmpmeta>${GPS}</x:xmpmeta>`)) : EMPTY,
  );
  return concatBytes(ascii("RIFF"), Uint8Array.of(...u32le(body.length)), body, options.trailer ? ascii(GPS) : EMPTY);
}
```

*Changed by amendment 8: the helper also exports `jpegSegment`, `exifBlock`, `pngChunk`, `pngFile`, `riffChunk`, `webpFile` and `VP8L_IMAGE`, and takes `extra`, `progressive`, `betweenScans` and `lossy`. The file is the reference.*

Create `lib/photos/signature.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { ascii, concatBytes, jpegBytes, pngBytes, webpBytes } from "@/tests/helpers/image-bytes";
import { photoTypeOf } from "./signature";

describe("photo signatures (selling spec 2.3)", () => {
  it("knows a JPEG, a PNG and a WebP by their first bytes", () => {
    expect(photoTypeOf(jpegBytes())).toBe("image/jpeg");
    expect(photoTypeOf(pngBytes())).toBe("image/png");
    expect(photoTypeOf(webpBytes())).toBe("image/webp");
  });

  it("refuses everything else, whatever the file was called", () => {
    expect(photoTypeOf(ascii("GIF89a"))).toBeNull();
    expect(photoTypeOf(ascii('<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg">'))).toBeNull();
    expect(photoTypeOf(concatBytes(Uint8Array.of(0, 0, 0, 0x18), ascii("ftypheic")))).toBeNull();
    expect(photoTypeOf(concatBytes(ascii("RIFF"), Uint8Array.of(4, 0, 0, 0), ascii("WAVE")))).toBeNull();
    expect(photoTypeOf(Uint8Array.of(0xff, 0xd8))).toBeNull();
    expect(photoTypeOf(new Uint8Array(0))).toBeNull();
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the stripper's test**

Create `lib/photos/strip-metadata.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { ascii, containsText, GPS, jpegBytes, pngBytes, webpBytes } from "@/tests/helpers/image-bytes";
import { stripMetadata } from "./strip-metadata";

describe("stripping a JPEG (selling spec 2.3, plan decision 11)", () => {
  it("drops Exif, XMP, IPTC and comments, and keeps the image, its tables and its colour profile", () => {
    const original = jpegBytes({ exif: true, xmp: true, iptc: true, comment: true });
    expect(containsText(original, GPS)).toBe(true);
    const stripped = stripMetadata(original, "image/jpeg")!;
    expect(containsText(stripped, GPS)).toBe(false);
    expect(stripped).toEqual(jpegBytes());
    expect(containsText(stripped, "ICC_PROFILE")).toBe(true);
  });

  it("gives back a photo with no metadata unchanged, and a second strip changes nothing", () => {
    expect(stripMetadata(jpegBytes(), "image/jpeg")).toEqual(jpegBytes());
    const once = stripMetadata(jpegBytes({ exif: true }), "image/jpeg")!;
    expect(stripMetadata(once, "image/jpeg")).toEqual(once);
  });

  it("reads past fill bytes, and drops whatever a phone appended after the image", () => {
    const stripped = stripMetadata(jpegBytes({ fill: true, trailer: true, exif: true }), "image/jpeg")!;
    expect(containsText(stripped, GPS)).toBe(false);
    expect(stripped).toEqual(jpegBytes());
  });

  it("refuses a JPEG whose structure doesn't add up", () => {
    const whole = jpegBytes({ exif: true });
    // No end marker.
    expect(stripMetadata(whole.subarray(0, whole.length - 2), "image/jpeg")).toBeNull();
    // Cut inside the Exif segment.
    expect(stripMetadata(whole.subarray(0, 30), "image/jpeg")).toBeNull();
    // No marker where one must be.
    expect(stripMetadata(Uint8Array.of(0xff, 0xd8, 0x12, 0x34), "image/jpeg")).toBeNull();
    expect(stripMetadata(ascii("not a photo"), "image/jpeg")).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe("stripping a PNG (selling spec 2.3, plan decision 11)", () => {
  it("drops eXIf and the text chunks, and keeps the image and its other chunks", () => {
    const original = pngBytes({ exif: true, text: true, ztxt: true, xmp: true });
    expect(containsText(original, GPS)).toBe(true);
    const stripped = stripMetadata(original, "image/png")!;
    expect(stripped).toEqual(pngBytes());
    for (const type of ["eXIf", "tEXt", "zTXt", "iTXt"]) expect(containsText(stripped, type), type).toBe(false);
  });

  it("stops at IEND, and gives back a photo with no metadata unchanged", () => {
    expect(stripMetadata(pngBytes({ trailer: true }), "image/png")).toEqual(pngBytes());
    expect(stripMetadata(pngBytes(), "image/png")).toEqual(pngBytes());
  });

  it("refuses a PNG that's cut short or has no end", () => {
    const whole = pngBytes();
    // IEND is the last 12 bytes.
    expect(stripMetadata(whole.subarray(0, whole.length - 12), "image/png")).toBeNull();
    expect(stripMetadata(whole.subarray(0, 40), "image/png")).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe("stripping a WebP (selling spec 2.3, plan decision 11)", () => {
  it("drops the EXIF and XMP chunks, clears their flags and works the RIFF size out again", () => {
    const original = webpBytes({ exif: true, xmp: true });
    expect(containsText(original, GPS)).toBe(true);
    const stripped = stripMetadata(original, "image/webp")!;
    expect(containsText(stripped, GPS)).toBe(false);
    expect(stripped).toEqual(webpBytes({ extended: true }));
  });

  it("gives back a simple WebP unchanged, and drops anything after the RIFF", () => {
    expect(stripMetadata(webpBytes(), "image/webp")).toEqual(webpBytes());
    expect(stripMetadata(webpBytes({ trailer: true }), "image/webp")).toEqual(webpBytes());
  });

  it("refuses a WebP whose sizes don't add up", () => {
    const whole = webpBytes({ exif: true });
    // The RIFF says more than there is.
    expect(stripMetadata(whole.subarray(0, whole.length - 3), "image/webp")).toBeNull();
    // VP8L (at byte 30, after the 12-byte header and the 18-byte VP8X) claims far more than the file holds.
    const lying = whole.slice();
    lying[37] = 0x7f;
    expect(stripMetadata(lying, "image/webp")).toBeNull();
  });
});
```
